    "text": "請根據我上載的文件內容進行專案記憶對齊：1. 嚴格依據文件事實提取顯式項目（Meeting、Objective、Requirement、User story、Task、UAT、Decision、Bottleneck、Milestone）；2. 忠實建立追溯關聯（保留缺層直連拓撲，嚴禁捏造不存在的層級）；3. 比對專案現有工單，僅對實質新條目執行建立，對相同條目保持現狀或增量更新。",
    "sender": "user",
    "timestamp": "下午02:10",
    "attachments": [
      {
        "name": "B_meeting_script_2.md",
        "size": 6580,
        "type": "text",
        "mimeType": "text/markdown",
        "textContent": "# 02 — Smart Queue Assistance Follow-up Meeting\n# Date: 2026-10-05\n# Time: 14:00–14:45\n# Channel: Microsoft Teams\n#\n# Participants:\n# Edmond — Project Lead\n# Karen — Business Owner / Customer Operations\n# Michael — IT / Technical\n# Rachel — UX / Service Design\n# Thomas — Airport Operations\n#\n# IMPORTANT TEST INSTRUCTION\n# Treat this transcript as raw conversational source evidence.\n# Do NOT treat it as a pre-structured project specification.\n# Do NOT assume that every statement is a Requirement, Task, Decision,\n# Milestone, User Story, UAT, Bottleneck, Charter, or other canonical item.\n# Preserve what is explicit, distinguish confirmed facts from tentative\n# statements, and allow existing Project Memory to determine whether this\n# is CREATE, UPDATE, NO_CHANGE, NEEDS_REVIEW, or CONFLICT.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nThanks everyone. This is our follow-up from the kickoff meeting.\nToday I mainly want to check what has changed since the first discussion,\nrather than recreate the project plan.\n\nKaren:\nSure. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT"
      }
    ]
  },

Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

          "candidateId": "CAND-004",
          "description": "# 專案章程 (Project Charter)\n\n| 欄位 (Field) | 說明與填寫內容 (Description) |\n|---|---|\n| Project Title | Projectson Phase 1 (Smart Queue Assistance) |\n| Business Sponsor | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Business Owner | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Project Lead | Edmond |\n| Problem & Opportunity | 旅客在航廈排隊時常面臨隊列識別困難，導致排錯隊情況發生，影響旅客體驗與機場營運效率。 |\n| Objectives | 開發並部署智能排隊輔助系統，協助 T1 航廈普通旅客快速且準確地識別應進入的隊列。 |\n| Quantifiable Benefits | 擬降低 30% 的錯誤排隊案例 (註：目前為擬議目標，待營運試行期間建立基準值後確認)。 |\n| Non-Quantifiable Benefits | 提升旅客自助服務體驗、減少前線人員引導壓力、優化航廈人流分佈。 |\n| Strategic Alignment | 提升機場數位化服務能力，優化旅客端到端的通關體驗。 |\n| Metric | 錯誤排隊案例減少率 (Wrong-queue reduction rate) |\n| Baseline | 待定 (將於營運試行階段收集) |\n| Target | 減少 30% (擬議目標) |\n| In-Scope | 1. 部署範圍：僅限於 Terminal 1 (T1) <br>2. 目標對象：僅限於普通旅客流 (Normal passenger flow) <br>3. 語言支持：中文、英文 <br>4. 功能：隊列識別建議、推薦理由說明、無法識別時的員工協助回退機制 (Staff-fallback) <br>5. 數據：整合隊列映射文件 (Queue mapping file) |\n| Out-of-Scope | 1. 其他航廈 (Other Terminals) <br>2. 特殊協助案例 (Special assistance cases) <br>3. 預估等待時間顯示 (Estimated waiting time) <br>4. 其他語言支持 (如日語、韓語) |\n| Project Team Members | Edmond (Project Lead), Karen (Business Owner), Michael (IT/Technical), Rachel (UX/Service Design), Thomas (Airport Operations) |\n| Stakeholders | Airport Operations, Customer Operations, IT Department, Terminal 1 Passengers |\n| Known Risks | 1. **技術集成風險**：實時隊列狀態數據 (Live queue-status) 的接口集成尚未驗證。 <br> 2. **合規風險**：隱私與數據保留審查 (Privacy and data-retention review) 尚未完成。 <br> 3. **性能風險**：3秒響應時間目前僅為技術目標，尚未確認為正式 SLA。 |\n\n**緩解方案**：\n- 由 Michael 優先執行隊列狀態集成驗證及安全隱私審查。\n- 將 3 秒響應時間視為驗證目標而非強制要求，待技術驗證後再定義 SLA。",
          "needsReview": false,
          "sectionTitle": "👥 會議記錄",

          "sectionTitle": "⚡ 執行任務",
          "sourceContent": "對當前設計進行技術壓力測試，驗證響應時間是否能穩定維持在 3 秒以下。",

            "excerpt": "性能目標驗證：3秒響應時間",

            "evidenceId": "EV-006",
            "sourceText": "# 02 — Smart Queue Assistance Follow-up Meeting\n# Date: 2026-10-05\n# Time: 14:00–14:45\n# Channel: Microsoft Teams\n#\n# Participants:\n# Edmond — Project Lead\n# Karen — Business Owner / Customer Operations\n# Michael — IT / Technical\n# Rachel — UX / Service Design\n# Thomas — Airport Operations\n#\n# IMPORTANT TEST INSTRUCTION\n# Treat this transcript as raw conversational source evidence.\n# Do NOT treat it as a pre-structured project specification.\n# Do NOT assume that every statement is a Requirement, Task, Decision,\n# Milestone, User Story, UAT, Bottleneck, Charter, or other canonical item.\n# Preserve what is explicit, distinguish confirmed facts from tentative\n# statements, and allow existing Project Memory to determine whether this\n# is CREATE, UPDATE, NO_CHANGE, NEEDS_REVIEW, or CONFLICT.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nThanks everyone. This is our follow-up from the kickoff meeting.\nToday I mainly want to check what has changed since the first discussion,\nrather than recreate the project plan.\n\nKaren:\nSure. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

            "description": "執行 Security side 的 privacy and data-retention review 以確保合規。",

            "assigneeName": "Michael",
            "evidenceType": "SOURCE_FACT",
            "reviewStatus": "CONFIRMED",
            "sourceContent": "執行 Security side 的 privacy and data-retention review 以確保合規。",

            "sourceEvidence": {
              "excerpt": "完成隱私與數據保留審查",
              "confidence": 1,
              "evidenceId": "EV-003",
              "sourceText": "# 02 — Smart Queue Assistance Follow-up Meeting\n# Date: 2026-10-05\n# Time: 14:00–14:45\n# Channel: Microsoft Teams\n#\n# Participants:\n# Edmond — Project Lead\n# Karen — Business Owner / Customer Operations\n# Michael — IT / Technical\n# Rachel — UX / Service Design\n# Thomas — Airport Operations\n#\n# IMPORTANT TEST INSTRUCTION\n# Treat this transcript as raw conversational source evidence.\n# Do NOT treat it as a pre-structured project specification.\n# Do NOT assume that every statement is a Requirement, Task, Decision,\n# Milestone, User Story, UAT, Bottleneck, Charter, or other canonical item.\n# Preserve what is explicit, distinguish confirmed facts from tentative\n# statements, and allow existing Project Memory to determine whether this\n# is CREATE, UPDATE, NO_CHANGE, NEEDS_REVIEW, or CONFLICT.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nThanks everyone. This is our follow-up from the kickoff meeting.\nToday I mainly want to check what has changed since the first discussion,\nrather than recreate the project plan.\n\nKaren:\nSure. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",
              "sourceType": "explicit",

            "itemType": "Charter",
            "itemTitle": "Projectson Phase 1 專案章程",

            "candidateId": "CAND-004",
            "description": "# 專案章程 (Project Charter)\n\n| 欄位 (Field) | 說明與填寫內容 (Description) |\n|---|---|\n| Project Title | Projectson Phase 1 (Smart Queue Assistance) |\n| Business Sponsor | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Business Owner | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Project Lead | Edmond |\n| Problem & Opportunity | 旅客在航廈排隊時常面臨隊列識別困難，導致排錯隊情況發生，影響旅客體驗與機場營運效率。 |\n| Objectives | 開發並部署智能排隊輔助系統，協助 T1 航廈普通旅客快速且準確地識別應進入的隊列。 |\n| Quantifiable Benefits | 擬降低 30% 的錯誤排隊案例 (註：目前為擬議目標，待營運試行期間建立基準值後確認)。 |\n| Non-Quantifiable Benefits | 提升旅客自助服務體驗、減少前線人員引導壓力、優化航廈人流分佈。 |\n| Strategic Alignment | 提升機場數位化服務能力，優化旅客端到端的通關體驗。 |\n| Metric | 錯誤排隊案例減少率 (Wrong-queue reduction rate) |\n| Baseline | 待定 (將於營運試行階段收集) |\n| Target | 減少 30% (擬議目標) |\n| In-Scope | 1. 部署範圍：僅限於 Terminal 1 (T1) <br>2. 目標對象：僅限於普通旅客流 (Normal passenger flow) <br>3. 語言支持：中文、英文 <br>4. 功能：隊列識別建議、推薦理由說明、無法識別時的員工協助回退機制 (Staff-fallback) <br>5. 數據：整合隊列映射文件 (Queue mapping file) |\n| Out-of-Scope | 1. 其他航廈 (Other Terminals) <br>2. 特殊協助案例 (Special assistance cases) <br>3. 預估等待時間顯示 (Estimated waiting time) <br>4. 其他語言支持 (如日語、韓語) |\n| Project Team Members | Edmond (Project Lead), Karen (Business Owner), Michael (IT/Technical), Rachel (UX/Service Design), Thomas (Airport Operations) |\n| Stakeholders | Airport Operations, Customer Operations, IT Department, Terminal 1 Passengers |\n| Known Risks | 1. **技術集成風險**：實時隊列狀態數據 (Live queue-status) 的接口集成尚未驗證。 <br> 2. **合規風險**：隱私與數據保留審查 (Privacy and data-retention review) 尚未完成。 <br> 3. **性能風險**：3秒響應時間目前僅為技術目標，尚未確認為正式 SLA。 |\n\n**緩解方案**：\n- 由 Michael 優先執行隊列狀態集成驗證及安全隱私審查。\n- 將 3 秒響應時間視為驗證目標而非強制要求，待技術驗證後再定義 SLA。",
            "needsReview": false,
            "evidenceType": "SOURCE_FACT",

Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

            "sourceContent": "# 02 — Smart Queue Assistance Follow-up Meeting\n# Date: 2026-10-05\n# Time: 14:00–14:45\n# Channel: Microsoft Teams\n#\n# Participants:\n# Edmond — Project Lead\n# Karen — Business Owner / Customer Operations\n# Michael — IT / Technical\n# Rachel — UX / Service Design\n# Thomas — Airport Operations\n#\n# IMPORTANT TEST INSTRUCTION\n# Treat this transcript as raw conversational source evidence.\n# Do NOT treat it as a pre-structured project specification.\n# Do NOT assume that every statement is a Requirement, Task, Decision,\n# Milestone, User Story, UAT, Bottleneck, Charter, or other canonical item.\n# Preserve what is explicit, distinguish confirmed facts from tentative\n# statements, and allow existing Project Memory to determine whether this\n# is CREATE, UPDATE, NO_CHANGE, NEEDS_REVIEW, or CONFLICT.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nThanks everyone. This is our follow-up from the kickoff meeting.\nToday I mainly want to check what has changed since the first discussion,\nrather than recreate the project plan.\n\nKaren:\nSure. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

            "candidateId": "CAND-004",
            "description": "# 專案章程 (Project Charter)\n\n| 欄位 (Field) | 說明與填寫內容 (Description) |\n|---|---|\n| Project Title | Projectson Phase 1 (Smart Queue Assistance) |\n| Business Sponsor | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Business Owner | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Project Lead | Edmond |\n| Problem & Opportunity | 旅客在航廈排隊時常面臨隊列識別困難，導致排錯隊情況發生，影響旅客體驗與機場營運效率。 |\n| Objectives | 開發並部署智能排隊輔助系統，協助 T1 航廈普通旅客快速且準確地識別應進入的隊列。 |\n| Quantifiable Benefits | 擬降低 30% 的錯誤排隊案例 (註：目前為擬議目標，待營運試行期間建立基準值後確認)。 |\n| Non-Quantifiable Benefits | 提升旅客自助服務體驗、減少前線人員引導壓力、優化航廈人流分佈。 |\n| Strategic Alignment | 提升機場數位化服務能力，優化旅客端到端的通關體驗。 |\n| Metric | 錯誤排隊案例減少率 (Wrong-queue reduction rate) |\n| Baseline | 待定 (將於營運試行階段收集) |\n| Target | 減少 30% (擬議目標) |\n| In-Scope | 1. 部署範圍：僅限於 Terminal 1 (T1) <br>2. 目標對象：僅限於普通旅客流 (Normal passenger flow) <br>3. 語言支持：中文、英文 <br>4. 功能：隊列識別建議、推薦理由說明、無法識別時的員工協助回退機制 (Staff-fallback) <br>5. 數據：整合隊列映射文件 (Queue mapping file) |\n| Out-of-Scope | 1. 其他航廈 (Other Terminals) <br>2. 特殊協助案例 (Special assistance cases) <br>3. 預估等待時間顯示 (Estimated waiting time) <br>4. 其他語言支持 (如日語、韓語) |\n| Project Team Members | Edmond (Project Lead), Karen (Business Owner), Michael (IT/Technical), Rachel (UX/Service Design), Thomas (Airport Operations) |\n| Stakeholders | Airport Operations, Customer Operations, IT Department, Terminal 1 Passengers |\n| Known Risks | 1. **技術集成風險**：實時隊列狀態數據 (Live queue-status) 的接口集成尚未驗證。 <br> 2. **合規風險**：隱私與數據保留審查 (Privacy and data-retention review) 尚未完成。 <br> 3. **性能風險**：3秒響應時間目前僅為技術目標，尚未確認為正式 SLA。 |\n\n**緩解方案**：\n- 由 Michael 優先執行隊列狀態集成驗證及安全隱私審查。\n- 將 3 秒響應時間視為驗證目標而非強制要求，待技術驗證後再定義 SLA。",

            "sourceContent": "# 專案章程 (Project Charter)\n\n| 欄位 (Field) | 說明與填寫內容 (Description) |\n|---|---|\n| Project Title | Projectson Phase 1 (Smart Queue Assistance) |\n| Business Sponsor | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Business Owner | Karen (Customer Operations) |\n| Project Lead | Edmond |\n| Problem & Opportunity | 旅客在航廈排隊時常面臨隊列識別困難，導致排錯隊情況發生，影響旅客體驗與機場營運效率。 |\n| Objectives | 開發並部署智能排隊輔助系統，協助 T1 航廈普通旅客快速且準確地識別應進入的隊列。 |\n| Quantifiable Benefits | 擬降低 30% 的錯誤排隊案例 (註：目前為擬議目標，待營運試行期間建立基準值後確認)。 |\n| Non-Quantifiable Benefits | 提升旅客自助服務體驗、減少前線人員引導壓力、優化航廈人流分佈。 |\n| Strategic Alignment | 提升機場數位化服務能力，優化旅客端到端的通關體驗。 |\n| Metric | 錯誤排隊案例減少率 (Wrong-queue reduction rate) |\n| Baseline | 待定 (將於營運試行階段收集) |\n| Target | 減少 30% (擬議目標) |\n| In-Scope | 1. 部署範圍：僅限於 Terminal 1 (T1) <br>2. 目標對象：僅限於普通旅客流 (Normal passenger flow) <br>3. 語言支持：中文、英文 <br>4. 功能：隊列識別建議、推薦理由說明、無法識別時的員工協助回退機制 (Staff-fallback) <br>5. 數據：整合隊列映射文件 (Queue mapping file) |\n| Out-of-Scope | 1. 其他航廈 (Other Terminals) <br>2. 特殊協助案例 (Special assistance cases) <br>3. 預估等待時間顯示 (Estimated waiting time) <br>4. 其他語言支持 (如日語、韓語) |\n| Project Team Members | Edmond (Project Lead), Karen (Business Owner), Michael (IT/Technical), Rachel (UX/Service Design), Thomas (Airport Operations) |\n| Stakeholders | Airport Operations, Customer Operations, IT Department, Terminal 1 Passengers |\n| Known Risks | 1. **技術集成風險**：實時隊列狀態數據 (Live queue-status) 的接口集成尚未驗證。 <br> 2. **合規風險**：隱私與數據保留審查 (Privacy and data-retention review) 尚未完成。 <br> 3. **性能風險**：3秒響應時間目前僅為技術目標，尚未確認為正式 SLA。 |\n\n**緩解方案**：\n- 由 Michael 優先執行隊列狀態集成驗證及安全隱私審查。\n- 將 3 秒響應時間視為驗證目標而非強制要求，待技術驗證後再定義 SLA。",

              "sourceText": "# 02 — Smart Queue Assistance Follow-up Meeting\n# Date: 2026-10-05\n# Time: 14:00–14:45\n# Channel: Microsoft Teams\n#\n# Participants:\n# Edmond — Project Lead\n# Karen — Business Owner / Customer Operations\n# Michael — IT / Technical\n# Rachel — UX / Service Design\n# Thomas — Airport Operations\n#\n# IMPORTANT TEST INSTRUCTION\n# Treat this transcript as raw conversational source evidence.\n# Do NOT treat it as a pre-structured project specification.\n# Do NOT assume that every statement is a Requirement, Task, Decision,\n# Milestone, User Story, UAT, Bottleneck, Charter, or other canonical item.\n# Preserve what is explicit, distinguish confirmed facts from tentative\n# statements, and allow existing Project Memory to determine whether this\n# is CREATE, UPDATE, NO_CHANGE, NEEDS_REVIEW, or CONFLICT.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nThanks everyone. This is our follow-up from the kickoff meeting.\nToday I mainly want to check what has changed since the first discussion,\nrather than recreate the project plan.\n\nKaren:\nSure. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

            "sourceText": "# 02 — Smart Queue Assistance Follow-up Meeting\n# Date: 2026-10-05\n# Time: 14:00–14:45\n# Channel: Microsoft Teams\n#\n# Participants:\n# Edmond — Project Lead\n# Karen — Business Owner / Customer Operations\n# Michael — IT / Technical\n# Rachel — UX / Service Design\n# Thomas — Airport Operations\n#\n# IMPORTANT TEST INSTRUCTION\n# Treat this transcript as raw conversational source evidence.\n# Do NOT treat it as a pre-structured project specification.\n# Do NOT assume that every statement is a Requirement, Task, Decision,\n# Milestone, User Story, UAT, Bottleneck, Charter, or other canonical item.\n# Preserve what is explicit, distinguish confirmed facts from tentative\n# statements, and allow existing Project Memory to determine whether this\n# is CREATE, UPDATE, NO_CHANGE, NEEDS_REVIEW, or CONFLICT.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nThanks everyone. This is our follow-up from the kickoff meeting.\nToday I mainly want to check what has changed since the first discussion,\nrather than recreate the project plan.\n\nKaren:\nSure. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",

            "sourceContent": "# 02 — Smart Queue Assistance Follow-up Meeting\n# Date: 2026-10-05\n# Time: 14:00–14:45\n# Channel: Microsoft Teams\n#\n# Participants:\n# Edmond — Project Lead\n# Karen — Business Owner / Customer Operations\n# Michael — IT / Technical\n# Rachel — UX / Service Design\n# Thomas — Airport Operations\n#\n# IMPORTANT TEST INSTRUCTION\n# Treat this transcript as raw conversational source evidence.\n# Do NOT treat it as a pre-structured project specification.\n# Do NOT assume that every statement is a Requirement, Task, Decision,\n# Milestone, User Story, UAT, Bottleneck, Charter, or other canonical item.\n# Preserve what is explicit, distinguish confirmed facts from tentative\n# statements, and allow existing Project Memory to determine whether this\n# is CREATE, UPDATE, NO_CHANGE, NEEDS_REVIEW, or CONFLICT.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nThanks everyone. This is our follow-up from the kickoff meeting.\nToday I mainly want to check what has changed since the first discussion,\nrather than recreate the project plan.\n\nKaren:\nSure. From the business side, the biggest update is that we now have\nsome preliminary queue mapping information from Airport Operations.\n\nThomas:\nYes. I sent Michael the latest queue mapping file yesterday.\nFor Terminal 1, we currently have the main passenger service queues\nidentified.\n\nMichael:\nI checked the file this morning.\nThe format is usable, but I still need to confirm whether we can get\nthe queue status data through the existing interface.\n\nEdmond:\nSo the queue mapping file itself is available, but the live queue-status\nintegration is still something you need to validate?\n\nMichael:\nCorrect.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. Please continue with that validation.\n\nMichael:\nI also checked the security side at a high level.\nThere doesn't appear to be an immediate issue, but I need to complete\nthe privacy and data-retention review before we can say that it is cleared.\n\nKaren:\nThat's fine. We don't need to call it a blocker at this stage.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Let's keep it as an outstanding check, not a blocker.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nI completed the interviews we discussed.\nI spoke with three passengers and two frontline staff.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. What did you find?\n\nRachel:\nThe general passenger flow we discussed still makes sense.\nOne thing that came through quite clearly is that passengers want to know\nwhy the system is recommending a particular queue.\n\nKaren:\nThat matches what we expected.\n\nRachel:\nThere was also a concern about what happens when the system cannot\nconfidently identify the correct queue.\n\nThomas:\nFrom the operations side, directing the passenger to staff assistance\nin that situation is acceptable.\n\nEdmond:\nGood. So the fallback we discussed remains valid.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nI also want to raise one thing from the business side.\nThe original idea about reducing wrong-queue cases by 30% is still useful\nas a target, but we still don't have a reliable baseline.\n\nEdmond:\nThen we shouldn't treat 30% as a confirmed KPI yet.\n\nKaren:\nAgreed.\n\nThomas:\nWe should probably collect the baseline during the operational trial.\n\nEdmond:\nThat's reasonable.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nThere is one technical point about response time.\nWe originally mentioned three seconds.\n\nEdmond:\nRight.\n\nMichael:\nI ran a very rough technical check.\nThe current design looks like it could potentially stay below three seconds,\nbut I don't think we should call that a confirmed performance requirement yet.\n\nEdmond:\nAgreed. Keep it as a technical target for validation.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nWhat about estimated waiting time?\nA few people asked whether the system could also tell passengers\nhow long they might need to wait.\n\nRachel:\nThat wasn't part of the interviews as a confirmed requirement.\n\nThomas:\nOperations also doesn't have enough confidence in the data yet.\n\nEdmond:\nThen let's keep estimated waiting time outside the initial release.\nIt can remain a future idea for now.\n\nKaren:\nOkay.\n\n---\n\nThomas:\nOne more operational point.\nFor the first release, let's keep this to normal passenger flow.\nSpecial assistance cases should continue to be handled by staff.\n\nEdmond:\nYes, that's still the Phase 1 scope.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nFor deployment, are we still talking about Terminal 1 only?\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Terminal 1 for the initial deployment.\nOther terminals can be considered later if Phase 1 works.\n\nThomas:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nRachel:\nWhat about language support?\nThe prototype currently assumes English and Chinese.\n\nKaren:\nThat's still what we need for the first release.\n\nEdmond:\nYes. Japanese and Korean are not commitments for Phase 1.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nI think the next technical action for me is clear:\nfirst validate the queue-status integration, then complete the\nsecurity and privacy review.\n\nEdmond:\nCorrect.\n\nRachel:\nAnd from UX, I don't think we need another round of interviews immediately.\nWe have enough initial feedback for the prototype.\n\nEdmond:\nOkay. No new interview task for now.\n\n---\n\nKaren:\nDo we need to change the requirements baseline date?\n\nEdmond:\nThe original target was October 2.\nWe're already past that date, and the requirements are still being refined.\n\nKaren:\nSo should we move it?\n\nEdmond:\nLet's not invent a new date today.\nWe'll confirm the revised date after the current validation work.\n\nThomas:\nMakes sense.\n\n---\n\nMichael:\nDoes that mean the prototype date of October 16 is still fixed?\n\nEdmond:\nNot necessarily.\nLet's keep October 16 as the previous tentative target until we review\nthe integration result.\n\nRachel:\nUnderstood.\n\n---\n\nEdmond:\nLet me summarize what I think we agreed today.\n\nThe project is still focused on helping normal Terminal 1 passengers\nidentify the appropriate queue.\n\nThe queue mapping information is now available, but live queue-status\nintegration still needs validation.\n\nThe security and privacy review is also still outstanding,\nbut neither issue is currently considered a blocker.\n\nRachel has completed the planned passenger and frontline interviews.\nThe feedback supports keeping the explanation and staff-fallback concepts.\n\nThe 30% reduction target remains a proposed target because we still\ndon't have a reliable baseline.\n\nThe three-second response time remains a technical target for validation,\nnot a confirmed SLA.\n\nEstimated waiting time stays outside the initial release as a future idea.\n\nPhase 1 remains Terminal 1, normal passenger flow, with Chinese and\nEnglish support.\n\nWe are not confirming a new requirements-baseline date or a new\nprototype date today.\n\nIs everyone okay with that?\n\nKaren:\nYes.\n\nMichael:\nYes.\n\nRachel:\nYes.\n\nThomas:\nYes.\n\nEdmond:\nGreat. That's all for today.\n\n---\n\n# END OF TRANSCRIPT",
